#7125. Product Builder at Camunda

Camunda is the enterprise platform for agentic orchestration, enabling organizations to coordinate AI agents, people, and systems across complex, end-to-end business processes. With built-in governance, auditability, and human oversight, Camunda gives enterprises the control they need to move AI from pilots to production — safely and at scale. Trusted by over 700 organizations worldwide, including 9 of top 10 US banks, Camunda helps enterprises boost operational efficiency, accelerate time-to-value, and deliver better customer experiences.Fully remote and global, we are in the middle of something bigger: transforming into an AI-first organisation, built on our own platform. We use Agentic AI to automate, orchestrate intelligent processes, and elevate human contribution across every team.Named GP Bullhound’s Top 100 Next Unicorn list, 2025 Great Place to Work certified. Visionary in 2025 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Business Orchestration and Automation Technologies. ranked 3rd in Flexa's 2026 Most Flexible Companies, We’re growing fast and looking for top talent to join our team. If you want meaningful work, visible impact and put something genuinely rare on your CV, keep reading.

Curious about the kind of challenges you'll work on at Camunda? Watch this quick 30-minute talk from our engineers to learn more about the new Camunda Exporter and how we’re solving complex problems at scale

We are looking for a Product Builder to join the IDP & RPA pod. You will take real problems from customers, decide what is worth solving, and build and ship the solution yourself, using AI tools to get from idea to production quickly without lowering the bar.

Product Building is how Camunda's Experience organization builds the applications people use on top of Camunda 8, such as Hub, Operate, and ProcessOS. Small pods of builders own a product area from customer problem to shipped change, with no hand-offs between product management, design, and engineering. We are setting this model up now, so you will help shape how it works.

The IDP & RPA pod builds how Camunda processes work with documents and legacy applications. With intelligent document processing (IDP), customers create extraction and classification templates in Camunda Hub and publish them so a process can use AI to turn invoices and other documents into structured data. With robotic process automation (RPA), customers integrate legacy applications that have no APIs, using Robot Framework scripts on a standalone RPA worker. The hard problems come with putting LLMs into customer processes: extraction quality, model and prompt choices, testing and trust, and working for AI agents as well as for people.

Help shape product direction for IDP & RPA by combining customer input, usage data, and field feedback into clear priorities.

Partner with the other builders and product strategy to turn problems into shipped features, using short written proposals and prototypes to align early.

Make judgment calls with incomplete information within your scope and own the outcome.

Communicate trade-offs and decisions clearly to stakeholders who don't live in the details the way you do.

Use AI tools across the whole loop, from specs to prototypes to production-ready code, and hold a high bar for what you ship by reviewing and improving the output rather than accepting it.

Build, test, and ship improvements to the IDP & RPA experience, reading errors and tracing issues far enough to give your pod useful context.

Talk to customers and to support and customer success colleagues about how IDP is used and where it breaks, and turn what you learn into decisions about what to build next.

Pick up customer incidents and trace them across Hub, the connectors, and the deployed process of the customer.

Track what you ship against real outcomes and adjust when the data tells you to.

Ability and/or willingness to use our product

We offer competitive, fair, and transparent compensation. Salary ranges are location-based, with Standard and Major markets (global tech hubs) reflecting local competition.

The Annual Total Target Cash (base salary + 100% variable target, where applicable) shown below spans from the minimum in a Standard market to the maximum in a Major market. #7123. Head of Ecosystem at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We aim to empower developers by providing a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We're looking for a Head of Ecosystem to lead the teams that make Supabase the backend for the platforms and tools developers already use. That covers AI app builders and coding agents, integrations with the developer tools teams rely on, and the Supabase Marketplace. You'll build this org from the ground up and shape how Supabase extends beyond its own platform.

You'll also drive hiring, onboarding, and career development while maintaining high engineering standards.

Build and lead a new engineering org across platform partners, integrations, and Marketplace, hiring EMs and engineers as it grows

Own the technical relationship with the platforms building on Supabase, from integration health to escalations

Set and maintain clear roadmaps that balance partner needs with platform priorities

Partner with Product, Partnerships, and Sales to define and execute our ecosystem strategy

Ship a unified, self-serve surface for integrations and Marketplace

Maintain high engineering standards: roadmap hygiene, issue tracking, on-call, incident response, and responsiveness to partners

Proven experience leading multiple engineering teams, including managing other managers

Experience building and running platform, API, or integration surfaces that third parties depend on in production

Technical credibility with partner engineering teams, and the judgment to say no

Experience building teams or an org from a small base, including hiring and developing engineering managers

Strong organizational and execution skills: setting roadmaps, driving accountability, and keeping teams moving under uncertainty

Experience managing remote, globally distributed teams

Excellent cross-functional communication skills, especially with Product and commercial teams

Have experience with OAuth, platform APIs, or app marketplaces

Have worked on a developer platform's partner or app ecosystem

Have worked with AI app builders or coding agents

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Every team member receives an annual education allowance to spend on learning—courses, books, conferences, or anything that supports your growth.

Supabase was born-remote and open-source-first. We believe our globally distributed team is our secret weapon in building tools developers love.

Over $1B raised (including our $500M Series F)

We move fast, build in public, and use what we ship. If it’s in your project, we probably use it in ours too. We believe deeply in the open-source ecosystem and strive to support—not replace—existing tools and communities.

#7119. Software Engineer - AI Tooling at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We’re looking for a Software Engineer to join our AI Tooling team and help shape how developers and agents build with Supabase. You'll build and own our AI tooling, including agents, the MCP server, agent skills and the dashboard assistant, and as the space moves you'll build the tooling for new technologies too. You'll build it all eval-first: every tool ships with evals and metrics, so we know, with evidence, that it makes agents better rather than worse.

You'll work closely with the Docs, CLI, Frontend and SDK teams, and with the AI Tooling Infrastructure engineers who run these services in production. Other teams contribute to AI Tooling to improve Supabase's agentic capabilities, and you'll help them do that while keeping the quality bar high.

This role is ideal for someone who thrives in async, fast-paced environments and is excited about building developer tools that scale to millions.

Build and improve the AI tooling we run today, including the MCP server, agent skills, agents and the Assistant.

Make new Supabase products usable by agents from day one: tools, skills and evals for new product surfaces. Also build tooling for new AI technologies as they emerge: prototype quickly, evaluate honestly, and decide with the team what is worth taking to production.

Drive an eval-first approach: build and improve evals, instrumentation and feedback loops, measure tools against a no-tool baseline, and help define the quality KPIs and gates that stop regressions shipping.

Understand how customers and agents actually use what we ship, and turn that into improvements to our tooling, interfaces and documentation.

Design how documentation and product knowledge are exposed to agents, and measure whether it works.

Build patterns and tools that help customers add AI to their own apps, such as MCP on Edge Functions, vector embeddings and agents running on Edge Functions.

Enable teams to improve Supabase's agentic capabilities through contributions to AI Tooling: clear contribution paths, templates, documentation and review practice.

Centralise shared AI logic so teams reuse it rather than duplicate it.

Ship tools that are battle-tested, with strong tests, benchmarking and stress testing where it counts.

Work with the AI Tooling Infrastructure engineers on reliability, auth and hosting.

Work across Docs, CLI, Frontend, SDK and other teams to deliver one coherent developer experience, and stay close to the fast-moving AI ecosystem.

Have 5+ years of experience in professional software engineering experience.

Write strong TypeScript and know the JavaScript ecosystem well: npm, ES modules, bundlers, compatibility.

Are deeply familiar with the AI ecosystem: current tools, agent patterns and practical trade-offs. You can tell a durable pattern from hype.

Have built developer tools, product interfaces or platform primitives used by other engineers.

Have designed or used evaluation frameworks to measure AI quality, reliability and task success, and understand baselines, variance and cost.

Have strong judgement about where AI improves workflows and how to avoid fragile or gimmicky experiences.

Write tests and validate reliability in real-world conditions.

Know Postgres and relational database practice.

Communicate clearly in writing, work across product, engineering and docs, and thrive in async, globally distributed teams.

Are comfortable navigating ambiguity and moving quickly.

Experience with vector databases and embeddings, ideally pgvector.

Examples of customer-facing AI features or agents you've shipped.

Examples of documentation or structured knowledge designed for developers or agents.

Experience with Braintrust or a similar eval and tracing platform, or setting up CI quality gates.

Open-source contributions or developer tooling you've maintained.

Experience across several product surfaces or teams.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

#7113. Information Security Engineer - Application Security at Camunda

Camunda is the enterprise platform for agentic orchestration, enabling organizations to coordinate AI agents, people, and systems across complex, end-to-end business processes. With built-in governance, auditability, and human oversight, Camunda gives enterprises the control they need to move AI from pilots to production — safely and at scale. Trusted by over 700 organizations worldwide, including 9 of top 10 US banks, Camunda helps enterprises boost operational efficiency, accelerate time-to-value, and deliver better customer experiences.Fully remote and global, we are in the middle of something bigger: transforming into an AI-first organisation, built on our own platform. We use Agentic AI to automate, orchestrate intelligent processes, and elevate human contribution across every team.Named GP Bullhound’s Top 100 Next Unicorn list, 2025 Great Place to Work certified. Visionary in 2025 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Business Orchestration and Automation Technologies. ranked 3rd in Flexa's 2026 Most Flexible Companies, We’re growing fast and looking for top talent to join our team. If you want meaningful work, visible impact and put something genuinely rare on your CV, keep reading.

Hi there! We're hiring an Information Security Engineer with a focus on Application Security to join our Information Security team at Camunda.

We are a team that takes security seriously without making it feel like a locked door. We work in a transparent, straightforward way, and we care about doing the work right rather than just checking a compliance box.

You will work side by side with our R&D engineers: reviewing designs, threat modeling new features, digging into code and helping teams make good, risk-based decisions about the vulnerabilities that come in. You'll also help build the internal tools that make this work scale, including our CVE registry and the LLM-assisted workflows we use to analyse code and triage findings.

Camunda is an AI-first company, and InfoSec is no exception: AI agents are part of how we review code, assess vulnerabilities, and build our tools. You will help shape how that works.

This is a hands-on role for someone who already has solid AppSec foundations and wants to grow. We are a generalist team, so you will also take part in our on-call rotation and incident response, and get exposure to other areas of security. But most of your time will be spent with engineers and building and maintaining internal tools and processes.

Vulnerability management: triage findings from SCA, container and code scanning, external reports and internal security testing; assess whether Camunda is actually affected; agree fix / mitigate / accept with engineering and follow through to release.

Threat modeling and security reviews: run threat modeling sessions and design reviews with R&D teams for new features and architecture changes, and turn the results into concrete, prioritised actions.

Code analysis with LLMs: use and improve LLM-assisted tooling (agents, prompts, reusable skills) to review code, validate findings and reproduce issues, while applying your own judgement to know when the model is wrong.

Build internal security tooling: contribute to the CVE registry and related automation that connects scanners, engineering teams, and customer communication. Much of it is built on Camunda itself.

Help engineers do the secure thing by default: answer questions, review pull requests, improve guidelines and occasionally run a session or workshop.

On-call and incident response: share the InfoSec on-call rotation with the other engineers and help investigate and resolve security incidents.

Support the wider team: help write security advisories for our customers, answer customer security questions and support audits.

Around 3+ years in security or software engineering, with at least 2 of them working on application or product security (AppSec, product security, pentesting, or a security-focused developer role all count).

Experience at a SaaS or software product company, working closely with engineering teams that ship often through modern CI/CD and cloud environments.

Hands-on vulnerability triage: you've worked with tools like Snyk, Trivy, Semgrep, CodeQL or similar, can tell a real issue from a false positive, and can explain how exploitable something actually is in context (CVSS, exploitability, configuration).

Threat modeling and design review experience (e.g. STRIDE) on real systems, ideally web apps, APIs, or distributed services.

You read and write code. You're comfortable reading Java (Camunda is built on it) well enough to follow a data flow and spot a security bug. You can write scripts and small tools and contribute to an existing codebase with review.

You build with AI, not just chat with it. You use AI coding agents (Claude Code, Cursor, Copilot or similar) in your daily work to write tools, analyse code or automate tasks. And you know their output still needs a second look, especially when it comes to security.

Clear, friendly communication with engineers. You see yourself as an enabler, not a gatekeeper, and you can explain risk to people who aren't security specialists.

Some Python and/or JavaScript experience, or experience building internal tools.

Willingness to learn and use Camunda, since we build a lot of our own security automation on it. Prior BPMN / workflow experience is a plus.

Experience responding to security incidents or being part of an on-call rotation.

Exposure to security advisories, CVE disclosure or bug bounty programmes.

Knowledge of AI/LLM security risks (OWASP Top 10 for LLMs / agentic apps, prompt injection, tool abuse).

Experience securing Kubernetes or container-based workloads, or open-source / B2B software.

We offer competitive, fair, and transparent compensation. Salary ranges are location-based, with Standard and Major markets (global tech hubs) reflecting local competition.

#7108. Senior Product Marketing Manager at Kit

Kit is the email marketing platform experts choose to grow their audience, deepen their relationships, and build a business around the work they love. For coaches, YouTubers, authors, podcasters, and other creatives, there isn't a better marketing hub to rely on to grow audiences, automate email marketing, and sell digital products — all within one platform.More importantly, there isn't a team more committed to helping creators earn a living. We're on a mission to help creators earn $1 billion using our creator marketing platform. We have always been 100% independent and 100% remote.We are proud to have built a product that our customers love, and we look for people who have enthusiasm and belief in our mission, vision, and values to join our team. We're also embracing AI thoughtfully — both in how we build and how we hire to ensure our team is adaptable, innovative, and ready for what's next.

Kit's most established creators run real companies, with teams, large lists, dedicated sending needs, and a buying process that our self-serve motion wasn't built to serve. Because this part of our business is growing, we're looking for a Senior Product Marketing Manager, Scaling Creators to own how Kit wins, lands, and expands these businesses through a sales-assisted motion that sits alongside our self-serve product.

You'll define who this buyer is, write the story that resonates and wins with them, and build the GTM strategy to drive inbound pipeline for our sales team. When these creators choose Kit and grow on it, they build more valuable businesses, and that's the outcome this role exists for.

This is a full-time individual contributor role ideal for someone who has added an upmarket sales-led motion to a product-led company before and wants their work measured in win rate and deal size.

You'll report to Christina Lhi, Head of Marketing, and work alongside two other experienced Product Marketing Managers on Kit's product marketing team. Together, you'll keep the self-serve and sales-assisted stories feeling like one Kit.

Our sales leadership, on who we sell to, the deal toolkit, enablement, and win/loss

Your work has a direct impact on helping Kit reach $100,000,000 ARR while protecting the self-serve engine that got us here.

#7102. Attorney, New Verticals at Atticus

At any given time, 16 million Americans are experiencing a crisis that requires urgent help from our legal system or government. The right assistance could transform their lives. But today, most never get it.

Atticus makes it easy for any sick or injured person in crisis to get the life-changing aid they deserve. In the last six years, we've become the leading platform connecting people with disabilities to government benefits. We’re also quickly scaling an injury practice to help victims of accidents, misconduct, and violence get compensation from insurance.

So far, we've helped hundreds of thousands of people access over $10B in life-changing aid — and earned over 20,000+ five-star reviews. And we're just getting started.

We've raised more than $100 million from top VC firms like Forerunner, GV (Google Ventures), and True Ventures, with ambitions to create a category defining business assisting Americans in need.

Each month, tens of thousands of Americans seek our help for a single legal matter – applying for Social Security Disability, filing a Workers’ Compensation claim – but many of these clients are actually eligible for additional aid without ever knowing it.

Your job as Lead Attorney, New Verticals is to help us do right by these clients, helping them claim additional benefits through the social safety net. You’ll oversee the team of intake specialists who speak with clients who have secondary claims, evaluating the case facts and whether to connect them with an Atticus attorney or local attorney to provide legal advice and potentially take on their case.

This is a job that straddles the line between practicing the law and growing a business. It requires developing a strong understanding of multiple fields of law, and being able to accurately identify when we can help and when we cannot. But even more importantly, this job requires you to be an incredibly strong operator: you need to keep the big picture in mind and stay laser-focused on how we can achieve our goals, oftentimes finding outside-the-box ways to accomplish them. You will report to our General Manager of New Verticals and work closely with our CEO and Managing Attorney.

On any given day, you’ll spend your time:

Speaking with severely injured clients trying to rebuild their lives after a catastrophic accident

Sourcing and vetting local counsel in individual markets, holding a high bar for representation quality and ending relationships that fall below it

Working with our data and insights team to identify existing clients who may have unrecognized claims in adjacent fields of law, and setting the standard for which ancillary cases we take on

Building the playbook for claim types we haven't handled before, starting by screening cases yourself and eventually creating the protocol our intake specialists will run at scale

This is a rare opportunity for an attorney to join a profitable and growing public-interest law firm and take on one of our biggest priorities as a company.

You can understand the nuances of various contingency fields of law and can apply them to a variety of cases (including Social Security Disability, Workers’ Compensation, Personal Injury, and more) – you don’t need to be an expert today, but you should be a fast learner.

You can empathize with anyone and quickly build rapport and relationships while exercising good judgment, both with our clients and local counsel at law firms across the country.

You have strong instincts and judgment as an operator – you can run effective and efficient processes, and you don’t let them run you.

You have a demonstrated commitment to doing good and helping people.

You’ve built a track record as a great people manager – you know how to motivate talent and hold a high bar for output.

You have meaningful work experience outside of serving as a lawyer, ideally at a startup or other fast-paced, agile environment (e.g., a business operations team).

We are strongly committed to building a diverse team. If you're from a background that's underrepresented in tech, we'd love to meet you.

This role offers a unique chance to join a startup that has strong traction (substantial funding, well-respected backers, tremendous growth, and many happy customers) but is still small enough that you can have a huge impact and play a role in shaping our success.

We're a certified B Corporation tackling a critical social problem. Our mission to help people in need drives everything we do, and your work here will touch many lives.

We offer competitive pay — including equity — and generous benefits:

Medical and dental insurance with 100% of employees and their families premiums covered

15 vacation days & ~20 paid holidays each year (including two weeks at end-of-year)

$1,000 reimbursable stipend for education and training outside of work

Up to $1,200/year student loan repayment assistance

Humble, thoughtful, smart, fun colleagues

We anticipate the base salary band for this role will be between $140,000 and $175,000 in addition to equity and benefits. The salary at offer will be determined by a number of factors such as candidate's experience, knowledge, skills and abilities, as well as internal equity among our team.

#7100. Trump taps DEI opponent to lead DOJ’s civil rights division

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

While at the U.S. Department of Education, Craig Trainor threatened to strip federal funding from colleges over their diversity initiatives.

Trainor’s selection signals that the Trump administration isn’t backing away from its heavy crackdown on colleges and their diversity initiatives. Trainor is set to replace Harmeet Dhillon, one of several top DOJ officials who stepped down after Democrats seized control of both chambers of Congress in the midterm elections.

Under Dhillon, the department’s civil rights division aggressively investigated colleges over pro-Palestinian protests and their policies for admissions, leading several high-profile colleges to cut deals with the Trump administration to end those probes.

The DOJ has even sued the University of California system, alleging that its Los Angeles campus hasn’t adequately responded to harassment toward Jewish and Israeli students.

The agency also sued Harvard University in February to force the Ivy League institution to hand over extensive admissions data and again in March to cut off its access to federal student aid. Judges dismissed both cases against Harvard, but the agency has since appealed the federal student aid case to the 1st U.S. Circuit Court of Appeals.

DOJ officials have recently stepped up their crusade against initiatives aimed at recruiting a diverse study body. So far, its investigations into admissions policies have led the department to accuse 10 medical and law schools of racially discriminating against White and Asian applicants.

Trainor has likewise spearheaded major efforts to stamp out DEI in the higher education sector. While serving as the Education Department’s acting assistant secretary for civil rights, Trainor threatened in February 2025 guidance to pull federal funding from colleges over DEI initiatives ranging from race-based scholarships to affinity graduation ceremonies.

Federal courts have since struck down the guidance. However, the DOJ released similar sweeping guidance last year that threatens to strip federal grant funding from colleges over what the agency deems as illegal DEI. DOJ officials flagged several commonplace practices as potentially unlawful, ranging from requiring job applicants to provide diversity statements to having identity-based lounges — even if they are open to all.

Trainor pursued similar anti-DEI policies in his most recent appointment as the U.S. Department of Housing and Urban Development’s assistant secretary for fair housing and equal opportunity. In that role, Trainor issued guidance warning colleges that affinity housing may violate federal law.

#7095. Engineering Manager - Pipelines at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We're looking for an Engineering Manager to lead the Pipelines team at Supabase. Supabase Pipelines is a managed change data capture product. It copies the tables you select from Supabase Postgres to other systems, then streams later changes. The product is in public alpha, with BigQuery, ClickHouse, DuckLake and Snowflake destinations.

Pipelines uses Postgres logical replication and Supabase ETL, our open source replication engine written in Rust. The team works close to the database. That means replication slots, WAL retention, initial table syncs, and the load they place on a customer's source database. The team also builds and runs the destination connectors. Correctness, ordering and recovery matter there as much as speed.

The Engineering Manager is a people, delivery and technical role. We don't expect you to write production code. We do expect you to join design discussions and help set the bar for engineering quality, testing and operations.

Build a healthy, high-trust team culture in a remote-first, globally distributed environment.

Run 1:1s, performance management, career development, and onboarding for your direct reports.

Handle people admin (leave, time off, etc.) for your team.

Own the hiring plan for your team and act as Hiring Manager. First round of interviewer for all roles on the team.

Partner with Talent Acquisition to source, interview, and close strong candidates.

Contribute to headcount planning, org design, and the evolution of engineering leadership at Supabase.

Own the team's roadmap and project management (Linear), driving accountability and clear milestones.

Manage workflow intake and Slack triage. These are the team’s primary inboxes for cross-team requests. You decide what gets routed where, prioritized, or pushed back on.

Own the team's weekly updates and keep stakeholders aligned without creating overhead.

Organize effort against support tickets and maintain the team's SLO.

Surface risks, dependencies, and tradeoffs early and clearly to leadership.

Train as an Incident Commander. Drive coordination, resolution, and post-incident follow-up.

Have 5+ years of engineering background, with enough depth to be credible with engineers building complex systems.

Have 3+ years managing remote engineering teams.

Have managed a team that built and ran data pipelines, change data capture, replication or streaming systems.

Have strong technical credibility in data systems. We look for delivery guarantees, ordering, idempotency, schema changes and backfills.

Know Postgres replication well, including logical replication, the WAL and replication slots, and how they affect a production database.

Know analytical destinations such as BigQuery, Snowflake, ClickHouse or lakehouse table formats like Iceberg.

Have led a team through production incidents on a system that holds customer data. You ran the follow-up work and kept the review blameless.

Have shipped a developer-facing data product to customers outside your company.

Have experience with Rust (not required but a plus)

Have managed remote, globally distributed teams that work asynchronously across disparate time zones.

Are technical enough to be credible with strong engineers, but genuinely passionate about people management. This is the work you want to do, not a track you're settling for.

Build psychological safety and a feedback culture. You give feedback often and in the moment, ask for it back, and create space for the team to do the same.

Operate as a multiplier. You help individuals achieve more than they could alone, and you don't need to be the best technical voice in the room.

Coach rather than direct. You remove blockers, connect people to context, and trust your engineers to own how the work gets done.

Have a real track record of performance management and can speak to specific examples of difficult conversations.

Are comfortable with ambiguity and can build structure where there isn't much.

Realtime lets developers send low-latency messages between clients, track and synchronize user state, and listen to database changes. Realtime is a globally distributed Elixir cluster built on Phoenix. The team balances low latency, fair limits between projects, and the cost of running the service. Customers expect connections to stay up, so reliability matters as much as new features.

#7082. International student enrollment fell 2.7% in 2025-26

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The annual Open Doors report also found signs losses are poised to continue, with the number of new foreign students plummeting 17.7% from the prior year.

U.S. colleges hosted roughly 1.1 million international students in the 2025-26 academic year, an annual decrease of 2.7%, according to the annual Open Doors report released Monday.

A 22.4% drop in international master’s enrollment, down to 237,655 students, helped drive the overall loss, the report found. Enrollment of foreign undergraduates stayed largely level — dipping just 0.3% to 356,165 — while the number of doctoral students increased 3.1% to 158,152.

The report, from the Institute of International Education and the U.S. Department of State, found signs of a weakening international enrollment pipeline that could lead to further losses in the coming years. New international enrollment — foreign students attending their institution for the first time — plummeted 17.7% from 2024-25, marking the second consecutive year of declines.

Alongside the Open Doors report, IIE released a preliminary fall survey of 809 colleges conducted with 10 higher education associations. They found that international enrollment declined 9% at surveyed institutions this fall, driven by a 15% drop in new foreign students and a 15% decrease in international graduate students.

Nearly 9 out of 10 surveyed colleges said the visa process has hurt foreign enrollment, and 70% blamed travel restrictions. Additionally, 60% cited competition from other countries for international students.

“There are more countries actively investing in and recruiting international students,” Mirka Martel, IIE’s head of research, evaluation and learning, said during a press call last week. “As a result, this often means that there are more destination opportunities.”

Just over half of the surveyed colleges that reported growth in new international student enrollment attributed the gains to recruitment efforts and campus athletics programs. Another 44% cited outreach efforts to foreign students who had already applied and been admitted.

Surveyed colleges also reported recruiting international students already within the U.S. Over half, 57%, said they reached out to foreign students studying at U.S. high schools, and 48% sought out international undergraduates for graduate programs.

The countries the U.S. has traditionally relied on for a steady stream of international students — namely, India and China — are sending fewer citizens to study here, according to the broader Open Doors report.

While India retained the top spot, sending 339,435 students in 2025-26, that’s a 6.5% decline from the year prior. China, whose college student population in the U.S. has ticked steadily downward since the pandemic, sent 254,458 students. That’s a 4.3% decrease from 2024-25.

Despite overall enrollment declines, the Open Doors report found an increase in Optional Practical Training participation. The program allows student visa holders to work in their field of study in the U.S. for up to three years following graduation. In 2025-26, 335,785 foreign students took part in OPT, up 14.1% from the previous year.

Martel said that the growth in OPT over the past four years is due to the post-pandemic rebound in international graduate students. Those students are still making their way through the OPT pipeline, she said.

However, early indicators suggest OPT participation could soon suffer declines as well. IIE’s preliminary fall poll, conducted in October, found that OPT students declined 6% year over year across surveyed colleges.

The drop comes amid news that OPT could get significantly more expensive for colleges.

Early last month, the U.S. Department of Homeland Security proposed up to $100,000 in new fees for each international student a college recommends for OPT.

Many colleges have told the Trump administration they won’t be able to afford the fee. However, the second Trump administration has largely enacted regulations affecting higher ed with few changes from its proposals.

The latest Open Doors data reinforces what many colleges and higher ed groups have flagged — that the international student pipeline is constricting.

A new analysis of federal data from the Presidents’ Alliance on Higher Education and Immigration found that fewer than 1.1 million international students were studying in the U.S. as of last month, down 6.7% from October 2024.

Miriam Feldblum, the organization’s president and CEO, called the data a warning sign for the U.S.

“The administration’s policies are creating uncertainty at every stage of an international student’s journey,” Feldblum said in a Thursday statement. “While we can’t attribute the decline to any single policy, the cumulative effect makes the United States a less predictable destination and puts our international talent pipeline at risk.”

In a separate survey of some 200 U.S. colleges released last week, nearly 6 out of 10 said they saw fewer international graduate students in fall 2026. The average reported decline was 13%.

Most respondents, 90%, said restrictive federal policies posed a significant obstacle to international enrollment, with some citing the colossal new fees the Trump administration has sought to impose on H-1B visas and the OPT program.

The Common App in August said that first-year international applicants using its platform declined 10% year over year during the 2025-26 cycle.

And public institutions across the country — including the University of Colorado, Pennsylvania State University, Central Michigan University and Ohio State University — have reported significant drops in international enrollment this fall. Nearly 6 in 10 international students in the U.S. enroll at public colleges, according to Martel.

You'll own the technical and analytical side of vendor management, reporting, efficiency, and the commercial interface across the company, while keeping procurement governance tight. Own vendor relationships: run our primary cloud and SaaS vendor relationships end-to-end (AWS, GCP, Cloudflare, CloudQuery, ClickHouse, and more), leading renewals and negotiations alongside Finance and Legal.

Run procurement governance: enforce our competitive-bid process for incoming requests and manage larger procurement through our managed-procurement partner, keeping oversight and accountability with FinOps.

People management: Build a healthy, high-trust team culture in a remote-first, globally distributed environment. Run 1:1s, performance management, career development, and onboarding for your direct report(s).Handle people admin (leave, time off, etc.) for your team.

Manage team’s delivery and accountability: Own the team's roadmap and project management (Linear), driving accountability and clear milestones. Manage workflow intake and Slack triage. Own the team's weekly updates and keep stakeholders aligned without creating overhead.

Drive commercial VIA: own spend visibility and unit economics, surface licensing and usage inefficiency before it becomes a surprise, and drive renewals, right-sizing, and tooling consolidation to realized savings.

Build reporting and governance: vendor and SaaS cost reporting is the unowned gap today and your highest-leverage area in the first year. Build the dashboards and reporting hands-on, partner with our data teams to scale them, and own the governance layer (spend policies, thresholds, accountability).

Coordinate the cloud efficiency program: keep optimization initiatives visible and tracked from commitment to realized savings, partnering with Engineering without taking over their delivery.

Have 8+ years in vendor/commercial management, procurement, FinOps, or technical/financial analysis, at a senior, function-owning level.

Have owned vendor relationships, contract negotiations, and renewals, and treat them as a lever on unit economics.

Have run procurement governance such as competitive-bid processes, benchmark analysis, approval thresholds, or managed-procurement partners.

Have high judgment and can hold a position and drive alignment without positional authority.

Build cost dashboards and reporting hands-on (SQL, BI tools), and are comfortable with vendor and cloud spend data (AWS, GCP, SaaS billing) and licensing/usage analysis.

Communicate clearly across both technical and non-technical audiences.

Have experience in async or globally distributed teams.

Are energized by building a function from the ground up and navigating ambiguity.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Every team member receives an annual education allowance to spend on learning—courses, books, conferences, or anything that supports your growth.

Supabase was born-remote and open-source-first. We believe our globally distributed team is our secret weapon in building tools developers love.

Over $1B raised (including our $500M Series F)

We move fast, build in public, and use what we ship. If it’s in your project, we probably use it in ours too. We believe deeply in the open-source ecosystem and strive to support—not replace—existing tools and communities.

#7068. Legal Assistant at Atticus

At any given time, 16 million Americans are experiencing a crisis that requires urgent help from our legal system or government. The right assistance could transform their lives. But today, most never get it.

Atticus makes it easy for any sick or injured American to get life-changing aid. Our mission is to tear down barriers between people in crisis and the aid they deserve. In the last six years, we’ve become the leading platform connecting people with disabilities to government benefits. We also help victims of accidents, misconduct, and violence get compensation from insurance.

So far, we’ve helped hundreds of thousands of people access over $10 billion in life-changing aid —and earned over 20,000 five-star reviews. And we’re just getting started.

We've raised more than $100 million from top VC firms like Forerunner, Google Ventures, and True Ventures, and we’re on our way to creating a category defining business assisting Americans in need.

Each day, thousands of people seek our help getting disability benefits or insurance compensation. They’re intimidated by the process, unsure where to turn, and desperate for good advice.

As a Legal Assistant at Atticus, your job is to ensure they get and stay on the right path. This job is different from that of a traditional legal assistant. The job mixes responsibilities in any good legal assistant’s comfort zone (build client relationships; support paralegals and attorneys in filing and managing claims) with those unique to being an operator in a fast-growing tech company (help Marketing source clients who’d be great in testimonials, or user test new tools for Product).

In a typical week you might do any of the following:

Make contact with a client that’s in danger of slipping through the cracks at SSA, and help get their case back on track.

Work with a client’s team to run down missing paperwork at SSA.

Take on an inefficient workflow that’s slowing the team down (e.g., processing case updates), scope an improvement, get team buy-in, and execute the improvement.

This role is a rare opportunity to join a fast-growing Series C startup and do meaningful social impact work. It’s designed to be a path to building expertise in the fields of law you support and taking on greater responsibilities over time (e.g. becoming a paralegal).

You have a Bachelor’s degree from a four-year university with strong academic performance.

You have experience in a professional role that required a substantial amount of customer-facing work and/or administrative tasks.

You are a resourceful team-player ready to dive into a scrappy startup environment where you will learn quickly and get your hands dirty.

You are comfortable in a variety of interpersonal conversations; you can be empathetic and kind when speaking with people in a moment of crisis, and can be thoughtful and organized when interacting with attorneys or the SSA.

You have a demonstrated commitment to doing good and helping people.

You have professional experience helping others apply for government benefits.

You have experience at a startup or other fast-paced, agile environment.

We are strongly committed to building a diverse team. If you’re from a background that’s underrepresented in tech, we’d love to meet you.

This is a rare opportunity to join a startup that has strong traction (substantial funding, well-respected backers, tremendous growth, and many happy customers) but is still small enough that you can have a huge impact and play a role in shaping our culture.

We’re a certified B Corporation tackling a critical social problem. Our mission to help people in need drives everything we do, and your work here will touch many lives.

We offer competitive pay — including equity for every employee — and generous benefits:

Medical and dental insurance with 100% of employees and their families premiums covered

15 vacation days & ~20 paid holidays each year (including two weeks at the end-of-year)

$1,000/year reimbursable stipend for education and training outside of work

Up to $1,200/year student loan repayment assistance

Humble, thoughtful, smart, fun colleagues

We anticipate the base salary band for this role will be between $50,000 and $75,000 in addition to equity and benefits. The salary at offer will be determined by a number of factors such as candidate’s experience, knowledge, skills and abilities, as well as internal equity among our team.

This job is fully remote and we’re committed to empowering everyone with flexibility. Work remotely, and travel to LA (on the company dime) as needed to be with your colleagues – usually quarterly, plus offsites. We care a lot about building a great culture and we think some interactions need to happen in person, so we put a lot of thought into retreats, offsites, and other ways to gather.

#7064. Public Policy Lead - US at Kindred

Kindred is a members-only home swapping platform that unlocks the ability to live a travel-rich lifestyle through the power of community. By exchanging primary residences with trusted peers, renters and owners alike can match with Kindred spirits and explore new destinations without breaking the bank.

Kindred is revolutionizing the “third option” of travel accommodation, compared to short-term rentals and hotels. Since launching in 2022, Kindred has grown to a global community of 300,000 members across 150+ cities and puts home swapping on the map as a radically more affordable, responsible, and connected way to travel.

To support that momentum, Kindred has raised over $150M in capital from some of the most credible investors in Silicon Valley, including Index Ventures, Andreessen Horowitz, New Enterprise Associates, Bessemer Venture Partners, Caffeinated Capital, Elad Gil, and founders of companies like Opendoor, Figma, ClassPass, and more.

Kindred’s co-founders are proven leaders from the early team at Opendoor ($15B+ exit), who have each built and scaled products that today generate $1B+ annual revenue combined.

We’re looking for the world’s top builders, executors, and believers to help us make travel more meaningful.

You can learn more about us in Forbes and The New York Times.

This is Kindred’s first dedicated public policy hire. You’ll join a three-person legal and policy team (reporting to the General Counsel) and own the execution of Kindred’s public policy and government relations efforts, with a focus on North American markets but covering additional markets as necessary.

The role is primarily focused on proactive advocacy to shape the policy environment for home-swapping. You’ll build relationships with elected officials, regulators, and industry groups. You’ll commission and manage economic impact research. You’ll develop city-level partnerships that normalize home-swapping as a category distinct from other travel categories. You’ll also develop working familiarity with the tax, housing, and accommodation regulations that intersect with Kindred’s model, and help parse how those frameworks apply (or don’t) to credit-based home-swapping. You’ll do all of this initially as a team of one, with outside consultants to support you and a path to building a team as the company grows and the function matures.

Execute Kindred’s public policy strategy with an emphasis on proactive advocacy.

Build and maintain relationships with elected officials, regulators, and staff at the local, state/provincial, and national levels. Represent Kindred in meetings, hearings, coalition convenings, and industry events.

Track proposed housing, short-term rental, tax, and platform regulations that could impact Kindred’s model, identifying where Kindred’s credit-based, non-commercial structure creates meaningful regulatory distinctions. Use AI-assisted tools and other technology to monitor developments across jurisdictions.

Further develop and evolve Kindred’s core policy narrative and positioning materials. Draft position papers, comment letters, testimony, and advocacy materials.

Propose and negotiate city-level partnerships that promote and normalize home-swapping. Build relationships with housing advocacy organizations, tourism boards, trade associations, and aligned industry coalitions.

Commission and manage third-party economic analyses on the impact of Kindred’s model, collaborating with internal business teams and outside consulting firms.

Identify, evaluate, and manage outside consultants cost-effectively (lobbyists, policy advisors, research firms).

Partner with internal teams to develop externally facing materials and data packages for stakeholder engagements.

Coordinate with the General Counsel on lobbying compliance, political activity disclosures, and regulatory filings.

7–10 years of experience in public policy, with at least 3 years at a technology company or similar fast paced environment.

Experience building or meaningfully contributing to building a public policy or government relations function, not just operating within an established one.

Track record of productive relationships with elected officials, regulators, and policy stakeholders.

Experience managing outside consultants, lobbyists, or policy advisors across multiple jurisdictions.

Strong written and verbal communication skills, including experience drafting policy position papers, advocacy materials, or regulatory comments.

Experience with policy matters at the city and state level in the United States, with an interest in policy work in new jurisdictions.

Fluency with AI tools and a willingness to integrate them into policy monitoring, research, and content development workflows.

Willingness to operate as an individual contributor while building the function, with the judgment to know when to engage outside support.

Experience in or adjacent to the sharing economy, home-sharing, travel, housing, or short-term rental sectors.

Experience at a growth-stage company (under 500 employees).

Familiarity with vacation/short-term rental, housing, and land use regulations, as well as related tax regulations.

Comfort with detailed regulatory analysis: ability to read and interpret statutes, ordinances, and administrative rules, and translate their implications into actionable policy positions. A law degree and legal practice experience is a plus but not required.

Build something new: You’ll help define the function and shape how Kindred engages with governments and regulators from the ground up.

A genuinely different model: Kindred’s credit-based, non-commercial home-swapping model is structurally distinct from existing travel lodging categories, contributing to sustainable tourism objectives.

Path to leadership: As the company and function grow, this role offers the opportunity to greater leadership.

Competitive compensation: Base plus meaningful equity in a growth-stage company.

At Kindred, we know that good things happen when we look out for one another. We offer a thoughtful, human-first benefits package designed to support health, wellbeing, and connection.

Our openings may span more than one career level. The base salary offered depends on factors such as experience, transferable skills, business needs, impact, and market data.

Flexible time off, plus 12 paid holidays per year

21 credits per year to stay in Kindred homes

$4,000 annual travel stipend to help offset travel-related costs

Remote-first and remote-flexible work environment, with opportunities to work from anywhere and gather in person, including at Kindred HQ in San Francisco

Regular team and company offsites to connect, collaborate, and build together

A vibrant, inclusive, and highly skilled team that ferociously protects team chemistry

A culture of high standards, feedback, and continual growth

We are committed to providing equal employment opportunities for all applicants and employees. Kindred does not discriminate on the basis of any protected characteristic, including race, color, ancestry, national origin, religion, creed, age, disability, sex, gender, sexual orientation, gender identity, gender expression, medical condition, genetic information, family care or medical leave status, marital status, domestic partner status, military and veteran status, or any other characteristic protected by US federal, state or local laws, or the laws of the country or jurisdiction where you work. Pursuant to the San Francisco Fair Chance Ordinance, we will consider for employment qualified applicants with arrest and conviction records.

For coaches, YouTubers, authors, podcasters, and other creatives, there isn't a better marketing hub to rely on to grow audiences, automate email marketing, and sell digital products — all within one platform.More importantly, there isn't a team more committed to helping creators earn a living. We're on a mission to help creators earn $1 billion using our creator marketing platform. We have always been 100% independent and 100% remote.We are proud to have built a product that our customers love, and we look for people who have enthusiasm and belief in our mission, vision, and values to join our team. We're also embracing AI thoughtfully — both in how we build and how we hire to ensure our team is adaptable, innovative, and ready for what's next.

Kit's most established creators run real companies, with teams, large lists, dedicated sending needs, and a buying process that our self-serve motion wasn't built to serve. Because this part of our business is growing, we're looking for a Senior Product Marketing Manager, Scaling Creators to own how Kit wins, lands, and expands these businesses through a sales-assisted motion that sits alongside our self-serve product.

You'll define who this buyer is, write the story that resonates and wins with them, and build the GTM strategy to drive inbound pipeline for our sales team. When these creators choose Kit and grow on it, they build more valuable businesses, and that's the outcome this role exists for.

This is a full-time individual contributor role ideal for someone who has added an upmarket sales-led motion to a product-led company before and wants their work measured in win rate and deal size.

You'll report to Christina Lhi, Head of Marketing, and work alongside two other experienced Product Marketing Managers on Kit's product marketing team. Together, you'll keep the self-serve and sales-assisted stories feeling like one Kit.

Our sales leadership, on who we sell to, the deal toolkit, enablement, and win/loss

Account Executives and Solutions Consultants, on demo narratives, discovery, and technical and migration proof

Product, on packaging, launches, and roadmap input

RevOps and analytics, on how accounts get routed to sales, attribution, and pricing decisions

Creator Success, on case studies, customer references, and expansion plays

Demand gen, content, and events, on campaigns and events for established creators

First Week: Dive into our onboarding curriculum in Notion and learn Kit's product, pricing, and current positioning. Attend Get-To-Know-You (GTKY) meetings with marketing, sales, Solutions Consultants, RevOps, and Creator Success. Get access to our CRM, call recordings, and product analytics, and start listening to recent sales calls. Understand how product marketing currently works at Kit

First Month: Shadow 15+ sales calls and talk with 10 established creators, including recent wins, losses, and churned accounts. Audit the sales content reps use today and how product usage currently routes accounts to sales. Set baselines with RevOps for win rate, sales cycle, deal size, and expansion revenue. Share a written point of view with the entire growth team on who our established creator buyer is and the three biggest gaps in how we sell to them. And an action plan on how we can win these creators.

First Year: Sales runs on messaging and tools you built, pitch with confidence and we drive 2X inbound MQLs from when you started. You’ll own and lead our channel strategy, setting the vision, driving cross-functional alignment, and partnering with Marketing to bring it to life. You'll run a quarterly win/loss program and share what you learn openly, build a clear path for sales leads on our website, and make sure launches that matter to larger accounts ship with both a self-serve and a sales-assisted version. Your work has a direct impact on helping Kit reach $100,000,000 ARR while protecting the self-serve engine that got us here.

Segmentation and buyer definition: turning firmographic and product usage data into a clear picture of who to sell to and when

Messaging and positioning for a sales-assisted buyer, including competitive differentiation and objection handling

Sales enablement that reps actually use: demo narratives, discovery guides, business cases, and battlecards

Writing and storytelling that turns product depth into a business case a non-technical buyer can act on

Working with CRM, product analytics, and pipeline data to measure funnel conversion, win rate, and deal size

Customer and market/competitor research, including win/loss interviews and call analysis

Systems thinking: connecting people, processes, data, and tools to identify opportunities and build repeatable solutions across the go-to-market motion

Strong AI fluency: knowing when to leverage AI and how to build AI-powered workflows or tools that scale product marketing work, with human judgment and quality checks built in

Pricing and packaging input for a new tier or add-on (nice to have)

7+ years in B2B SaaS product marketing, including at least 2 years supporting a sales team selling to mid-market and/or larger accounts

Added a sales-led motion on top of a product-led one at a high-growth company, and can show what changed before and after with clear metrics

Built a deal toolkit and enablement program from little or nothing, and tied it to a sales metric like win rate or deal size

Ran a win/loss or customer research program that changed how a team sold or built

Ran an expansion or account-based program for an existing customer base (nice to have)

Worked in email, martech, or the creator economy

You lead without a formal title. You align sales, product, RevOps, and finance on goals, trade-offs, and what success looks like, then keep things moving.

You advocate for creators in decisions, and you protect the self-serve experience instead of pushing every account toward sales.

You act with urgency and stay focused on hitting OKRs and owning outcomes.

You work in public. You share findings, drafts, and the reasoning behind decisions so others can build on them.

You connect data to decisions, and you help others get sharper about using it.

You raise the bar on quality and iterate toward it, and you shape how your team uses AI, from call analysis to keeping battlecards current.

Experience in only self-serve or only enterprise sales. This role lives in the space between them, and you'll need to understand both.

Treating enablement as a deck handoff. The work includes coaching reps, measuring what gets used, and revising what doesn't land.

Wanting a sales playbook already in place. Much of this motion doesn't exist yet, and you'll be building it as you learn.

Preferring to be measured on output rather than outcomes. You'll share accountability with Sales for win rate, deal size, and expansion revenue.

Needing extensive approval before acting. You'll be trusted to make calls on speed versus quality and to explain your reasoning.

Discomfort with direct, candid feedback, especially from sellers on what's working in live deals.

We're 100% remote with team members across the globe. We work mostly on East Coast time—so if you're based on the West Coast of the United States, Europe, or Asia, you might have a few early mornings or late evenings. We do our best to keep things reasonable, but a little time zone juggling comes with the territory.

Kit has standardized salaries based on position, no matter where you live. For this role, we’re hiring at our level 4 ($166,000). Level is determined based on experience and our interview process.

We cover up to $2,280 per month toward medical premiums, with dental and vision premiums fully covered. We offer Health Insurance plans through Aetna

$2,000 equipment allowance for your first two years, $1,000 budget every following two years. Company-provided laptops are issued to every Kit team member and are not included in the equipment budget

Individual learning + development budget ($3,500/year)

Creator meetup budget ($50/month) to connect with creators over coffee, lunch, or other in-person meetups

Childcare benefit up to $3,000 annually through JOON

Twenty (20) days of paid time off during each year of employment

Paid paid vacation: An after-tax bonus of $1,000 for taking five consecutive days of vacation where you’re fully unplugged from work

Two weeks of paid sick time each year, including mental health + well being days

Twelve (12) weeks paid parental leave and flexible scheduling in your child’s first year

Up to six weeks of paid bereavement leave, medical leave, and disaster after six months of employment, two weeks of each paid leave in your first six months

Winter Break Closure: Kit closes for a week at the end of December, giving everyone a collective break to enjoy the holiday season. Essential support services remain available, with teams coordinating to ensure coverage during this period

Four-week, paid sabbatical after five years with the team

Fantastic in-person or virtual retreats with the team twice a year

Position: Product & Website Development SpecialistSalary: $2,200 - 2,500 AUD/monthWorking Hours & Conditions: 9:00AM to 5:00PM AEST; Mondays to FridaysHolidays: AU Holidays

This is a hands-on, technically focused role combining website design, web development, advanced Excel product management and catalogue integration.

The business is looking to modernise its existing website and develop two distinct online platforms: one focused on a subscription-based offering and another showcasing its corporate manufacturing capabilities and branding.

The successful candidate will take ownership of translating complex Excel-based product information into accurate, functional and professionally designed websites.

This is not a general administration, customer service or order-processing role. We are looking for someone with strong technical capabilities who can independently manage both website development and large-scale product data.

The final website platforms and technical requirements will be confirmed during recruitment.

We are looking for someone who is both technically capable and creatively minded.

This is an excellent opportunity for an experienced website and product data specialist to take ownership of a significant digital development project within an established Australian business.

Rather than simply maintaining an existing website, you will have the opportunity to shape, build and improve two distinct digital platforms, while creating the systems that support their ongoing success.

We are seeking someone who can combine technical expertise, creative problem-solving and strong attention to detail to make a genuine impact on the business.

#7044. Rigorous course participation rose after 2021 law, University of Illinois report finds

Let Higher Ed Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

Increases in dual credit and Advanced Placement participation in Illinois were particularly notable for Black students and students with disabilities.

Previous research shows links between success in programs like dual credit and AP, and greater college access and persistence.

Statewide, participation in dual credit courses rose by 17.1% overall in 2023-24 and another 9.1% in 2024-25, as compared with 1.6% and 6.9% in the two previous school years, respectively. Statewide participation in AP courses rose 6.6% in both 2023-24 and 2024-25, as compared with 2.3% the previous year and a participation drop of 5.9% in 2021-22.

While these and other gains were made in the immediate aftermath of the legislation, the study did not attempt to prove a causal relationship, said Sarah Cashdollar, associate director of the Illinois Workforce and Education Research Collaborative.

“Many other things are happening in the state that could be responsible,” she said, including amendments to the state’s Dual Credit Quality Act and higher state appropriations for dual credit and AP courses. “The state, the legislature, and all those who support it are throwing a lot at this issue in trying to improve participation in programs,” she said. “It’s hard for researchers to disentangle the effects of each of them separately.”

Nonetheless, the data describes a positive trend, said Meg Bates, IWERC director. “Certainly, it would have been a disappointment to see it go in the other direction,” she said. We’re excited to see it, and we’ll keep watching it. Hopefully we can do that kind of causal study in the future.”

The IWERC study bolsters other research that shows enrollment in rigorous courses is critical for underrepresented populations, Bates said.

For dual credit, the study found participation rates in 2023-24 increased from the previous year by 28.4% for Latino students and 25.6% for Black students. The year-over-year participation rate increase for Asian students was 15.6% and 13.9% for White students.

For AP, the increases were 9.2% for Latino students, 8.5% for Black students, 4.6% for White students and 3.4% for Asian students.

“Sometimes, we see a general trend that looks one way, but when you go underneath the hood, changes in certain students’ participation” runs counter to the trend, she said. “In this case, we got to see a little bit of both.”

However, she added, “That’s great, seeing more Black and Latino students, and more English learners. But because it’s going up for everyone, some of our gaps are widening.”

Those gaps should spur further research, Bates said, because educators and policymakers don’t want to see student experiences become more asymmetrical. “Following up with districts to understand the ‘why’ is very important.”

Cashdollar would like to see more research into the spikes in participation for English learners and Latino students.

“Was this group previously overlooked despite being capable of taking these classes — so the policy did what it was intended to do, and qualify more students?” she said. “A better understanding of all the different factors at play would be really useful.”

Bates believes a policy like automatic enrollment is uniquely equipped to target English learners. “Educating English learners can be costly for districts and can be hard to staff because you need bilingual educators,” she said.

This is a "nudge" to include English learners in this coursework," Cashdollar said.

"That’s a huge takeaway and something advocates are very excited about.”

#7035. Trump administration puts end to mandated boost in Head Start pay

Let K-12 Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

In a new final rule, HHS is rolling back a Biden-era effort to keep salaries competitive and reduce high staff turnover in the early education program.

The Trump administration is ending efforts to boost pay and benefits for Head Start employees, according to a final rule from the U.S. Department of Health and Human Services scheduled to be published Monday. Those efforts would have seen most Head Start teachers earn an additional $10,000 in annual salaries to align with pay for other public preschool educators.

The final rule reverses a 2024 Biden-era rule that supporters said would have helped Head Start early childhood education programs recruit and retain qualified staff and provide consistent quality programming for children and families. The 2024 rule for increased wages and benefits would have been fully implemented in 2031.

Instead, the final rule set to publish on Monday removes those requirements, calling the directive "costly and overly prescriptive for Head Start programs and staff." HHS' Administration for Children and Families, which administers the Head Start program, said its final rule will yield more than $2 billion in cost savings as it restores "greater flexibility to local Head Start programs."

Removing the wage and benefit requirements will prevent the elimination of 106,000 Head Start classroom slots.

Average Head Start teacher salaries range from $26,964 in Mississippi to $79,853 in the District of Columbia, according to a 2024 Urban Institute brief.

The change is just one part of a broader, ongoing effort by the Trump administration to overhaul the Head Start program.

The National Head Start Association, a nonprofit organization that represents Head Start families, providers and educators, said in a Friday statement that underfunding is straining the program's ability to pay staff competitively while maintaining student enrollment levels.

NHSA also said that removing federal wage and benefit requirements "does not change competitive pressures or eliminate the staffing shortages that continue to limit some programs' ability to open classrooms and serve more eligible children and families."

NHSA Executive Director Yasmina Vinci said in a statement, "A strong Head Start program depends on a stable, qualified workforce, and programs must have the resources necessary to recruit, retain, and support the professionals who serve children and families every day."

In a statement responding to the final rule, the National Association for the Education of Young Children said, "HHS frames this as a choice between paying educators and serving children, but a funded slot without a qualified teacher serves no one."

The final rule is to take effect 60 days after publication.
